<div class='question question'>

A number machine has produced the following table of input and output numbers.

|input|output|
|:--: |:--:  |
|$-1$ |$1$   |
|$0$  |$1.5$ |
|$2$  |$2.5$ |
|$5$  |$4$   |

Write suitable labels on the diagram below.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-1/section-1-question-21-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-1/section-1-question-21-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-1/section-1-question-21-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the input $= x$

Let the rule $= a \times x + b$

From Row 1, where $x = -1$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times -1 + b &= 1 \\\\
b - a &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

From Row 2, where $x = 0$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 0 + b &= 1.5 \\\\
b &= 1.5
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\',

$
\begin{aligned}
b - a &= 1 \\\\
1.5 - a &= 1 \\\\
a &= 0.5
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the rule $= 0.5 \times x + 1.5$

Lets verify,

From Row 3, where $x = 2$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.5 \times 2 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 1 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 2.5 = 2.5
\end{aligned}
$

From Row 4, where $x = 5$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.5 \times 5 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 2.5 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 4 = 4
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the labels are **$\times 0.5$** , **$+ 1.5$**

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\times 0.5, + 1.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the input $= x$
<emptyline>
Let the rule $= a \times x + b$
<emptyline>
From Row 1, where $x = -1$
<emptyline>
$

$
<emptyline>
From Row 2, where $x = 0$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 0 + b &= 1.5 \\\\
b &= 1.5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve for \'a\',
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
b - a &= 1 \\\\
1.5 - a &= 1 \\\\
a &= 0.5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore, the rule $= 0.5 \times x + 1.5$
<emptyline>
Lets verify,
<emptyline>
From Row 3, where $x = 2$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.5 \times 2 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 1 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 2.5 = 2.5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
From Row 4, where $x = 5$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.5 \times 5 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 2.5 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 4 = 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore, the labels are **$\times 0.5$** , **$+ 1.5$**
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

&= 83 \ \text{millimetres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2015--group-1:1:36</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>

tnumber
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

To find the digital root of a number, you add the digits repeatedly until you reach a single digit number.

For example, the digital root of $169$ is $7$ because $1 + 6 + 9 = 16$, and $1 + 6 = 7$

The digital roots of the first $9$ square numbers are given in the table below:

|             |$1^\text{2}$|$2^\text{2}$|$3^\text{2}$|$4^\text{2}$|$5^\text{2}$|$6^\text{2}$|$7^\text{2}$|$8^\text{2}$|$9^\text{2}$|
|:-----------:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
|square number|$1$ |$4$ |$9$ |$16$|$25$|$36$|$49$|$64$|$81$|
|digital root |$1$ |$4$ |$9$ |$7$ |$7$ |$9$ |$4$ |$1$ |$9$ |

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Complete the table of digital roots of the next nine square numbers.

|             |$10^\text{2}$|$11^\text{2}$|$12^\text{2}$|$13^\text{2}$|$14^\text{2}$|$15^\text{2}$|$16^\text{2}$|$17^\text{2}$|$18^\text{2}$|
|:-----------:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
|square number|$100$ |$121$ |$144$|$169$ |$196$|$225$|$256$|$289$|$324$|
|digital root | | | |$7$ | | | | |$9$ |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 100 &= 1 + 0 + 0 \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 121 &= 1 + 2 + 1 \\\\
&= 4
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 144 &= 1 + 4 + 4 \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 196 &= 1 + 9 + 6 \\\\ 
&= 16 \\\\
&= 1 + 6 \\\\
&= 7
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 225 &= 2 + 2 + 5 \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 256 &= 2 + 5 + 6 \\\\
&= 13 \\\\
&= 1 + 3 \\\\
&= 4
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 289 &= 2 + 8 + 9 \\\\
&= 19 \\\\
&= 1 + 9 \\\\
&= 10 \\\\
&= 1 + 0 \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

1, 4, 9, 7, 9, 4, 1

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 100 &= 1 + 0 + 0 \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 121 &= 1 + 2 + 1 \\\\
&= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 144 &= 1 + 4 + 4 \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

&= 16 \\\\
&= 1 + 6 \\\\
&= 7
\end{aligned}
$

$

&= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$

&= 13 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

&= 19 \\\\
&= 1 + 9 \\\\
&= 10 \\\\
&= 1 + 0 \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
9 + (5 \times 7)  &= 47 - a \\\\
9 + 35            &= 47 - a \\\\
44                &= 47 - a\\\\
a                &= 47 - 44 \\\\
a                 &= 3 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\boxed{\phantom{11}}  \ \times 16 = 36 \div 9$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 16  &= 36 \div 9 \\\\
a \times 16  &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {36} {9 \times 16} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9 \times 4 \times 4} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {9} \times 4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 16  &= 36 \div 9 \\\\
a \times 16  &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {36} {9 \times 16} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9 \times 4 \times 4} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {9} \times 4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

$16 \times 7  \ \boxed{\phantom{11}} \ 17 \times 6$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$16 \times 7  \quad \boxed{\green{>}} \quad 17 \times 6$

Lets verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Left}       &= 16 \times 7 \\\\
                  &= 112 \\\\
\text{Right}      &= 17 \times 6 \\\\
                  &= 102
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$16 \times 7  \ \boxed{>} \ 17 \times 6$

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

Work out the input when the output is $120$.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-15-subquestion-1--subsubquestion-2--00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-15-subquestion-1--subsubquestion-2--00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-15-subquestion-1--subsubquestion-2--00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the input $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 7) \times 4        &= 120 \\\\
a + 7                   &= \dfrac{120}{4} \\\\
a + 7                   &= \dfrac{30 \times 4}{4} \\\\
a + 7                   &= \dfrac{30 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
a + 7                   &= 30 \\\\
a                       &= 30 - 7 \\\\
a                       &= 23
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

23

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the input $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 7) \times 4        &= 120 \\\\
a + 7                   &= \dfrac{120}{4} \\\\
a + 7                   &= \dfrac{30 \times 4}{4} \\\\
a + 7                   &= \dfrac{30 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
a + 7                   &= 30 \\\\
a                       &= 30 - 7 \\\\
a                       &= 23
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>23
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>
</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

There are two possible function machines that will give the results shown below.

Work out the rules for the two possible machines.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-15-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-15-subquestion-2-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1/section-1-question-15-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

**Rule 1**

Let first label $= + a$

Let second label $= \times b$

$
\begin{aligned}
(5 + a) \times b &= 4 \\\\
5b + ab &= 4 \\\\
(8 + a) \times b &= 10 \\\\
8b + ab &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
8b + ab &= 10 \\\\
8b + (4 - 5b) &= 10 \\\\
8b - 5b &= 10 - 4 \\\\
3b &= 6 \\\\
b &= \dfrac{6}{3} \\\\
b &= \dfrac{2 \times 3}{3} \\\\
b &= \dfrac{2 \times \cancel 3}{\cancel 3} \\\\
b &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
5b + ab &= 4 \\\\
5 \times 2 + 2 \times a &= 4 \\\\
10 + 2a &= 4 \\\\
2a &= 4 - 10 \\\\
2a &= -6 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{-6}{2} \\\\
a &= -3
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore,

The rule $= - 3 \ \text{then}\ \times 2$

**Rule 2**

Let first label $= \times a$

Let second label $= + b$

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times a + b &= 4 \\\\
5a + b &= 4 \\\\
8 \times a + b &= 10 \\\\
8a + b &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
8a + b &= 10 \\\\
8a + (4 - 5a) &= 10 \\\\
8a - 5a &= 10 - 4 \\\\
3a &= 6 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{6}{3} \\\\
a &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
5a + b &= 4 \\\\
5 \times 2 + b &= 4 \\\\
10 + b &= 4 \\\\
b &= 4 - 10 \\\\
b &= -6
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore,

The rule $= \times 2 \ \text{then}\ - 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-3 \ \text{then}\ \times 2 \\\\ \text{Or,}\ \times 2 \ \text{then}\ - 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>**Rule 1**
<emptyline>
Let first label $= + a$
<emptyline>
Let second label $= \times b$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(5 + a) \times b &= 4 \\\\
5b + ab &= 4 \\\\
(8 + a) \times b &= 10 \\\\
8b + ab &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve for \'b\'
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
8b + ab &= 10 \\\\
8b + (4 - 5b) &= 10 \\\\
8b - 5b &= 10 - 4 \\\\
3b &= 6 \\\\
b &= \dfrac{6}{3} \\\\
b &= \dfrac{2 \times 3}{3} \\\\
b &= \dfrac{2 \times \cancel 3}{\cancel 3} \\\\
b &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve for \'a\'
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5b + ab &= 4 \\\\
5 \times 2 + 2 \times a &= 4 \\\\
10 + 2a &= 4 \\\\
2a &= 4 - 10 \\\\
2a &= -6 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{-6}{2} \\\\
a &= -3
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore,
<emptyline>
The rule $= - 3 \ \text{then}\ \times 2$
<emptyline>
**Rule 2**
<emptyline>
Let first label $= \times a$
<emptyline>
Let second label $= + b$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times a + b &= 4 \\\\
5a + b &= 4 \\\\
8 \times a + b &= 10 \\\\
8a + b &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve for \'a\'
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
8a + b &= 10 \\\\
8a + (4 - 5a) &= 10 \\\\
8a - 5a &= 10 - 4 \\\\
3a &= 6 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{6}{3} \\\\
a &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets solve for \'b\'
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5a + b &= 4 \\\\
5 \times 2 + b &= 4 \\\\
10 + b &= 4 \\\\
b &= 4 - 10 \\\\
b &= -6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Therefore,
<emptyline>
The rule $= \times 2 \ \text{then}\ - 6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$-3 \ \text{then}\ \times 2 \\\\ \text{Or,}\ \times 2 \ \text{then}\ - 6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

The cards can be placed side by side to form different numbers.

For example, using four of the cards, the largest $4$-digit even number that can be made is $9854$.

$\Huge{\boxed{9} \ \boxed{8} \ \boxed{5} \ \boxed{4}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the smallest $5$-digit multiple of $5$ that can be made?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$13485$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13485$

</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$38 \times 42$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 38 \times 42 \\\\
&= (37 + 1) \times 42 \\\\
&= 37 \times 42 + 1 \times 42 \\\\
&= 1554 + 42 \\\\
&= 1596
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1596$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
&= 38 \times 42 \\\\
&= (37 + 1) \times 42 \\\\
&= 37 \times 42 + 1 \times 42 \\\\
&= 1554 + 42 \\\\
&= 1596
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$37 \times 21$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 37 \times 21 \\\\
&= 37 \times \dfrac {42} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {37 \times 42} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1554} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {777 \times 2} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {777 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
&= 777
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$777$

\end {aligned}

</div>
<div class='question question'>

Zoya has a jug of capacity $1.5 \ \text{litres}$ which is full of lemonade.

She has eight cups each of capacity $150 \ \text{ml}$.

Zoya fills the cups with lemonade from her jug.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What volume of lemonade is left in the jug?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1.5 \ \text{liter}\ - 150 \ \text{ml}\ \times 8 \\\\
&= (1.5  \times 1000) \ \text{ml}\ - 150 \ \text{ml}\ \times 8 \\\\
&= 1500 - 1200 \\\\
&= 300 \ \text{ml}\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

300 ml

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1.5 \ \text{liter}\ - 150 \ \text{ml}\ \times 8 \\\\
&= (1.5  \times 1000) \ \text{ml}\ - 150 \ \text{ml}\ \times 8 \\\\
&= 1500 - 1200 \\\\
&= 300 \ \text{ml}\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>300 ml
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Zoya has five $2 \ \text{litre}$ bottles of lemonade

How many times could Zoya completely fill her jug from these bottles?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total volume Zoya has} &= 5 \times 2 \text{litre} \\\\
&= 10 \text{litre}
\end{aligned}
$

Let number of times jug can be filled $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 1.5 &= 10 \\\\
a \times \dfrac{15}{10} &= 10 \\\\
a \times \dfrac{3}{2} &= 10 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{10}{\dfrac{3}{2}} \\\\
a &= 10 \times \dfrac{2}{3} \\\\
a &= \dfrac{20}{3} \\\\
a &= 6.66
\end{aligned}
$

Since the jug had to be completely filled,

Total times $= 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total volume Zoya has} &= 5 \times 2 \text{litre} \\\\
&= 10 \text{litre}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let number of times jug can be filled $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 1.5 &= 10 \\\\

a &= \dfrac{10}{\dfrac{3}{2}} \\\\
a &= 10 \times \dfrac{2}{3} \\\\
a &= \dfrac{20}{3} \\\\
a &= 6.66
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Since the jug had to be completely filled,
<emptyline>
Total times $= 6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-2:1:14</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tnumber
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Lisa thinks of her favourite number.

She multiplies her favourite number by $2$, subtracts $3$ and gets $19$.


What is Lisa's favourite number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Lisa's favourite number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times a - 3      &= 19 \\\\
2a                  &= 19 + 3 \\\\
2a                  &= 22 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {22} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {11 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                   &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

Lisa's favourite number $= 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let Lisa's favourite number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times a - 3      &= 19 \\\\
2a                  &= 19 + 3 \\\\
2a                  &= 22 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {22} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {11 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                   &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lisa's favourite number $= 11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

                            &= 20
\end{aligned}

<div class='question question'>

In order to convert from the imperial units ounces and pounds to the metric unit kilograms, you should use the following conversions:

$
\begin{aligned}
16 \ \text{ounces} &= 1 \ \text{pound}\\
2.2 \ \text{pounds} &= 1 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

Newborn tiger cubs weigh about $56 \ \text{ounces}$.

Circle the mass in kilograms which gives the best approximation of the mass of a newborn tiger cub.

$0.5 \ \text{kg} \quad 1 \ \text{kg} \quad 1.5 \ \text{kg} \quad 2 \ \text{kg} \quad 2.5 \ \text{kg} \quad 3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 56 \ \text{ounces} \\\\
&= (56 \div 16) \ \text{pounds} \\\\
&= 3.5 \ \text{pounds} \\\\
&= (3.5 \div 2.2) \ \text{kg} \\\\
&= 1.59 \ \text{kg} \\\\
&= 1.5 \ \text{kg} \ \text{(approx.)}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

1.5 kg

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 56 \ \text{ounces} \\\\
&= (56 \div 16) \ \text{pounds} \\\\
&= 3.5 \ \text{pounds} \\\\
&= (3.5 \div 2.2) \ \text{kg} \\\\
&= 1.59 \ \text{kg} \\\\
&= 1.5 \ \text{kg} \ \text{(approx.)}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

What is the difference between the product of $4$ and $11$ and the difference of $4$ and $11?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>



$
\begin{aligned}
&= (4 \times 11) - (4 - 11)  \\\\
&= 44 - (-7) \\\\
&= 44 + 7 \\\\
&= 51
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$51$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= (4 \times 11) - (4 - 11)  \\\\
&= 44 - (-7) \\\\
&= 44 + 7 \\\\
&= 51
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>09B08DAF-540F-4115-B9CB-3DA13E21C2F3</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-manchester-grammar-school--11-plus--maths--2010--arithmetic-1:1:6</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tnumber
</li>
<li>
tmoney
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Neil lends Rodger $\pounds 4$. Rodger pays back $30 \%$ of this the next day. How much does he still owe Neil?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>



Let's convert pounds to pence.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total amount} &= \pounds 4 \\\\
&= 4 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
&= 400 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

Rodger pays back $30 \%$. So,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount paid} &= 30\% \text{ of } 400 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30} {100} \times 400 \\\\
&= 30 \times 4 \\\\
&= 120  \ \text{p} 
\end{aligned}
$

Let's calculate the amount Rodger still owes Neil.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount owed} &= 400 - 120 \\\\
&= 280 \ \text{p} \\\\
&= \pounds \dfrac{280}{100} \\\\
&= \pounds 2.80
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Can we do better than the previous solution? 

If Rodger has paid back $30\%$ of the money, he still has $70\%$ to pay. 
So we can directly calculate the amount owed and save on calculating the amount paid as well.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount owed} &= 70\% \text{ of } 400 \\\\
&= \dfrac {70} {100} \times 400 \\\\
&= 70 \times 4 \\\\
&= 280 \ \text{p} \\\\
&= \pounds \dfrac{280}{100} \\\\
&= \pounds 2.80
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2.80$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
<emptyline>
Let's convert pounds to pence.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total amount} &= \pounds 4 \\\\
&= 4 \times 100 \ \text{p} \\\\
&= 400 \ \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Rodger pays back $30 \%$. So,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount paid} &= 30\% \text{ of } 400 \\\\
&= \dfrac {30} {100} \times 400 \\\\
&= 30 \times 4 \\\\
&= 120  \ \text{p} 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's calculate the amount Rodger still owes Neil.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount owed} &= 400 - 120 \\\\
&= 280 \ \text{p} \\\\
&= \pounds \dfrac{280}{100} \\\\
&= \pounds 2.80
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Can we do better than the previous solution? 
<emptyline>
If Rodger has paid back $30\%$ of the money, he still has $70\%$ to pay. 
So we can directly calculate the amount owed and save on calculating the amount paid as well.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount owed} &= 70\% \text{ of } 400 \\\\
&= \dfrac {70} {100} \times 400 \\\\
&= 70 \times 4 \\\\
&= 280 \ \text{p} \\\\
&= \pounds \dfrac{280}{100} \\\\
&= \pounds 2.80
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2.80$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A *preble* is a set of three numbers in which the last number is the product of the first two numbers, for example $(3, 2, 6)$.

Work out the missing number in each of these prebles.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per question, 

$
\begin {aligned}
6 \times 1 \dfrac {1}{3} &= a \\\\
6 \times \dfrac {4}{3} &= a \\\\
\dfrac {24}{3} &= a \\\\
a &= 8
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$a = 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per question, 
<emptyline>
$

6 \times \dfrac {4}{3} &= a \\\\

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per question,

$
\begin {aligned}
0.4 \times e &= 0.24 \\\\
\dfrac {4} {10} \times e &= \dfrac {24} {100} \\\\
e &= \dfrac {24} {100} \div \dfrac {4} {10} \\\\
e &= \dfrac {24} {100} \times \dfrac {10} {4} \\\\
e &= \dfrac {24} {10\cancel{0}} \times \dfrac {\cancel{10}} {4} \\\\
e &= \dfrac {6} {10} \\\\
e &= 0.6
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down a fraction which is between $\dfrac{1}{3}$ and $\dfrac{1}{2}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number between $\dfrac{1}{3}$ and $\dfrac{1}{2}$ = $a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a &= Average( \dfrac{1}{3}, \dfrac{1}{2} ) \\\\
  &= ( \dfrac{1}{3} + \dfrac{1}{2} ) \div 2 \\\\
  &= ( \dfrac{1 \times 2}{3 \times 2} + \dfrac{1 \times 3}{2 \times 3} ) \div 2 \\\\
  &= ( \dfrac{2}{6} + \dfrac{3}{6} ) \div 2 \\\\
  &= ( \dfrac{2 + 3}{6}) \div 2 \\\\
  &= ( \dfrac{5}{6}) \div 2 \\\\
  &= \dfrac{5}{6 \times 2} \\\\
a &= \dfrac{5}{12}
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{5}{12}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number between $\dfrac{1}{3}$ and $\dfrac{1}{2}$ = $a$

  &= ( \dfrac{2}{6} + \dfrac{3}{6} ) \div 2 \\\\
  &= ( \dfrac{2 + 3}{6}) \div 2 \\\\
  &= ( \dfrac{5}{6}) \div 2 \\\\
  &= \dfrac{5}{6 \times 2} \\\\
a &= \dfrac{5}{12}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='workings'>

What is $\text{FAB }11$ divided by $\text{FAB } 9?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {\text {FAB} \ 11} {\text {FAB} \ 9} \\\\
&= \dfrac {11 \times 10 \times \text {FAB} \ 9} {\text {FAB} \ 9} \\\\
&= \dfrac {11 \times 10 \times \cancel {\text {FAB} \ 9}} {\cancel {\text {FAB} \ 9}} \\\\
&= 11 \times 10 \\\\
&= 110
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$110$

&= 110
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

What is the product of the sum of $6$ and $3$ with the difference between $2$ and $10?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= (6 + 3) \times (2 - 10) \\\\
&= 9 \times (-8) \\\\
&= -72
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\begin {aligned}
&= (6 + 3) \times (2 - 10) \\\\
&= 9 \times (-8) \\\\
&= -72
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

What is the total when you add together all the factors of $72$ (including $1$ and $72$ itself)?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Factors of} \ 72  &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 9, 12, 18, 24, 36, 72 \\\\
\text {Sum}              &= 1 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 6 + 8 + 9 + 12 + 18 + 24 + 36 + 72 \\\\
                         &= 195
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$195$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}

\text {Sum}              &= 1 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 6 + 8 + 9 + 12 + 18 + 24 + 36 + 72 \\\\
                         &= 195
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
